  <div  class="popup-content">
    
<form class="search-form" role="search" method="get" id="" action="https://www.group-ib.com/">
  <input type="search" placeholder="Search" name="s" id="search-input" value=""/>
  <button type="submit" class="search-button"><img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/search-icon.svg" alt="Search Icon"></button>
</form>   </div>

<style>
  .hidden {
    display: none;
  }
</style>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
  const openSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.open-search-popup')];
  const closeSearchPopupBtns = [...document.querySelectorAll('.close-search-popup')];
  const searchPopup = document.querySelector('.search-popup');

  const closePopup = () => {
    searchPopup.style.display = 'none';
    openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.add('hidden'));
  };

  openSearchPopupBtns.forEach(openSearchPopupBtn => {
    openSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', (e) => {
      e.preventDefault();

      searchPopup.style.display = 'block';
      openSearchPopupBtn.classList.add('hidden');
      closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(btn => btn.classList.remove('hidden'));
    });
  });

  closeSearchPopupBtns.forEach(closeSearchPopupBtn => {
    closeSearchPopupBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
      closePopup();
    });
  });

  document.addEventListener('click', (event) => {
    if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target) && !openSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target)) && !closeSearchPopupBtns.some(btn => btn.contains(event.target))) {
      setTimeout(() => {
        if (!searchPopup.contains(event.target)) {
          closePopup();
        }
      }, 100);
    }
  });
</script>    </div>

                                                <div class="author__wrapper">
                                                    <img src="/wp-content/themes/gib-theme/assets/images/GIB-round-white.png" alt="Connecting the Bots" class="author__image">                                                    <div class="author__about">

                            <h1 class="header__title title-h2 mb-24 mt-md-48 mt-lg-64 mt-xl-64">Connecting the Bots</h1><p class="header__text mb-xs-48 mb-64 mb-lg-96 mb-xl-96">Hancitor fuels Cuba Ransomware Operations</p><div class="post-info__date mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><div class="body2">May 7, 2021 · <span id="reading-time"></span> min to read · Ransomware</div></div><div class="post-info__image mb-24 mb-lg-32 mb-xl-48"><picture><source srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-55.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img data-no-lazy="" fetchpriority="high" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/img-1440-55.jpg" alt="" class="webpexpress-processed"></picture></div><div class="post-info__tags"><div class="post-info__tag body2">Cuba</div><div class="post-info__tag body2">Ransomware</div></div>                        </div>
                    </div>
                </div>
                <div class="blog-post-content" id="blog-post-content">
                    <div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>The <strong>Hancitor</strong> downloader has been around for quite some time already. It is known since at least 2016 for dropping Pony and Vawtrak. As a loader, it has been used to download other malware families, such as Ficker stealer and NetSupport RAT, to compromised hosts. Its operators also showed interest in post exploitation activities, deploying Cobalt Strike Beacon on the hosts located in Active Directory environments. After a few unremarkable and quiet years, Hancitor resurfaced again — it decided to join the Big Game Hunting.</p>
<p>Hancitor became another commodity malware which partnered with ransomware gangs to help them gain initial access to target networks – the increasing trend outlined by Group-IB researchers in the recent Ransomware Uncovered 2020/2021 report.</p>
<p>Group-IB Threat Intelligence team found that Hancitor is being actively used by the threat actors to deploy Cuba ransomware. <strong>Cuba ransomware</strong> has been active since at least January 2020. Its operators have a DLS site, where they post exfiltrated data from their victims who refused to pay the ransom. As of April 28, the site mentioned nine companies primarily from aviation, financial, education and manufacturing industries. Hancitor&#8217;s deep interest in Big Game Hunting is further supported by <a href="https://medium.com/walmartglobaltech/man1-moskal-hancitor-and-a-side-of-ransomware-d77b4d991618" rel="nofollow">Jason Reaves</a>&#8216;s earlier findings about Hancitor&#8217;s association with the Zeppelin ransomware.</p>
<p>The blog post examines a typical Hancitor and Cuba kill chain, the threat actors&#8217; TTPs, detailed recommendations, and mitigation techniques.</p>
<p>Usually, Hancitor is distributed via spam campaigns. Such emails are disguised to look like DocuSign notifications:</p>
<div id="attachment_26040" style="width: 629px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-17.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26040" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26040 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20619%20320'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="An example of spam email content" width="619" height="320" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-17.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26040" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26040 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/1-min-17.png" alt="An example of spam email content" width="619" height="320"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26040" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 1. An example of spam email content</p></div>
<p>Clicking the malicious link obviously leads to downloading a weaponized document. As always, the document contains instructions on how to remove &#8220;protection&#8221;:</p>
<div id="attachment_26041" style="width: 612px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26041" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26041" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20602%20304'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="The contents of weaponized document - hancitor" width="602" height="304" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-6.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26041" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26041" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/2-min-6.webp" alt="The contents of weaponized document - hancitor" width="602" height="304" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26041" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 2. The contents of weaponized document</p></div>
<p>In recent campaigns, if the content is enabled, the macros extracts and drops Hancitor DLL to <em>C:\Users\%username%\AppData\Roaming\Microsoft\Word</em>, and runs it via <em>rundll32.exe</em>.</p>
<p>Such behavior is easy detectable by host-based defenses as winword.exe should not normally start <em>rundll32.exe</em>:</p>
<div id="attachment_26042" style="width: 1154px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26042" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26042" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201144%20353'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt=" detecting abnormal activity caused by Hancitor" width="1144" height="353" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-6.webp" /><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26042" decoding="async" class="size-full wp-image-26042" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/3-min-6.webp" alt=" detecting abnormal activity caused by Hancitor" width="1144" height="353" /></noscript><p id="caption-attachment-26042" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 3. Group-IB Managed XDR Huntpoint detecting abnormal activity caused by Hancitor</p></div>
<p>Data received from C2 server is base64-encoded and XORed with 0x7A. After decoding and decrypting, the received command is checked. The command should be presented as one of the following symbols: «b», «e», «l», «n», «r». If it&#8217;s supported, Hancitor does one of the following actions:</p>
</div>
                            
                            <div class="blog-post-content__section"><div class="table"><div class="table__wrapper"><table width="100%" data-table-width="20%;80%">
<thead>
<tr>
<th width="20%">Command Code</th>
<th width="80%">Description</th>
</tr>
</thead>
<tbody>
<tr>
<td width="20%">b</td>
<td width="80%">Downloads a PE from the server, which address was received from C2. Downloaded data is decrypted, decompressed and injected to newly started svchost.exe process.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">e</td>
<td width="80%">Downloads a PE from the server, which address was received from C2. Downloaded data is decrypted, decompressed and executed in separate thread of the Hancitor process.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">l</td>
<td width="80%">Downloads a PE from the server, which address was received from C2. Downloaded data is decrypted, decompressed and injected to newly started svchost.exe process via creation of the remote thread.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">n</td>
<td width="80%">Looks like an equivalent of the ping command.</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td width="20%">r</td>
<td width="80%">Download a PE from the server, which address was received from C2. Downloaded data is decrypted, decompressed and saved to a temporary file. If downloaded file is an EXE file, it is executed via CreateProcess, if downloaded file is a DLL file, it is executed via rundll32.exe.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div></div></div><div class="blog-post-content__section--text"><p>One of the most common payloads delivered by Hancitor these days is Ficker stealer, which is actively advertised on various underground forums and is capable of extracting data from various web-browsers, mail clients, cryptocurrency wallets, etc. However, Cobalt Strike usage deserves more attention.</p>
<p>During the post-exploitation phase, the threat actors rely mostly on Cobalt Strike, leveraging its capabilities on various stages of attack lifecycle.</p>
<p>From execution perspective, just like many other ransomware operators, they used jump psexec and jump psexec_psh, and relied heavily on SMB Beacons, commonly using generic pipe names. In some cases, they also used less common techniques, such as WMI and WinRM to execute the Beacon stagers on remote hosts.</p>
<p>As Cobalt Strike has credential dumping capabilities, the threat actors leverage mimikatz&#8217;s sekurlsa::logonpasswords. At the same time, in some cases they use a separate binary to run mimikatz on some hosts. This tool is also used for enabling lateral movement capability with obtained hashes and mimikatz&#8217;s sekurlsa::pth.</p>
<p>The Beacon&#8217;s capabilities were also used to scan the compromised network. In addition, the group leveraged some custom tools for network reconnaissance. The first tool is called Netping – it&#8217;s a simple scanner capable of collecting information about alive hosts in the network and saving it into a text file, the other tool, Protoping, to collect information about available network shares. Built-in tools were also abused. For example, adversary used net view command to collect information about the hosts in the network and nltest utility to collect information about the compromised domain.</p>
<p>Besides Cobalt Strike&#8217;s capabilities to run the Beacon stagers on remote hosts, the attackers used Remote Desktop Protocol to move laterally. They have a batch script called rdp.bat in their arsenal, which is used to enable RDP connections and add corresponding firewall rule on the target host. Similar scripts were observed to be used by <a href="https://www.group-ib.com/blog/prolock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">ProLock</a> and <a href="/resources/research-hub/egregor-ransomware/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">Egregor</a> operators.</p>
<p>Ficker stealer wasn&#8217;t the only publicly advertised tool in the threat actors&#8217; arsenal. Another tool, which is becoming more and more popular among various ransomware operators – SystemBC. Such additional backdoors allowed the attackers to download and execute additional payloads even if Cobalt Strike activity was detected and blocked.</p>
<p>The approach to ransomware deployment is quite trivial, but still effective. Like many others, the threat actors usually leveraged PsExec for deployment.</p>
<p>The exfiltrated data is published on a dedicated Cuba DLS (Data Leak Site).</p>
<p><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-26043 size-full" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201428%20866'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="cuba data leak site" width="1428" height="866" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-6.webp" /><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone wp-image-26043 size-full" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/4-min-6.webp" alt="cuba data leak site" width="1428" height="866" /></noscript></p>
<p>As of April 28, the website offers to download data for free from 9 mainly US companies from the aviation, financial, education, manufacturing, and logistics companies which refused to pay the ransom. The actual number of victims is expected to be higher.</p>
<p>An interesting feature of the site is that it also includes the paid content section:</p>
<div id="attachment_26044" style="width: 977px" class="wp-caption alignnone"><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-23.png.webp" type="image/webp"><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26044" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26044 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20967%20286'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="Paid content featured on Cuba's DLS" width="967" height="286" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-23.png"><noscript><img aria-describedby="caption-attachment-26044" decoding="async" class="wp-image-26044 size-full webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/5-min-23.png" alt="Paid content featured on Cuba's DLS" width="967" height="286"></noscript></picture><p id="caption-attachment-26044" class="wp-caption-text">Figure 4. Paid content featured on Cuba&#8217;s DLS</p></div>
<p>Cuba ransomware samples that Group-IB DFIR team observed wasn&#8217;t very sophisticated, and even didn&#8217;t have Windows Shadow Copies removing functionality, so the threat actors had to have additional scripting capabilities.</p>
<p>Files are encrypted using ChaCha20 with 12-bytes length IV. The keys are encrypted with RSA-4096 algorithm. The RSA implementation is likely copied from the following <a href="https://github.com/wolfSSL/wolfssl" target="_blank" rel="nofollow noopener">repository</a>.</p>
<p>According to Group-IB TI&amp;A, the group behind ransomware deployments is Balbesi. Despite the fact the group is leveraging quite common techniques in their operations, their attacks are still quite effective and affects organizations from various sectors, including financial, pharmaceutical, educational, industrial, professional services and software development, focusing mainly on Europe and USA.</p>
<p>Below you can find both MITRE ATT&amp;CK mapping and corresponding mitigations list.</p>
<p><picture><source data-lazy-srcset="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-6.jpg.webp" type="image/webp"><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26039 webpexpress-processed" src="data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%201200%201414'%3E%3C/svg%3E" alt="MITRE ATT&CK mapping and corresponding mitigations list - hancitor" width="1200" height="1414" data-lazy-src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-6.jpg"><noscript><img decoding="async" class="alignnone size-full wp-image-26039 webpexpress-processed" src="https://www.group-ib.com/wp-content/uploads/6-min-6.jpg" alt="MITRE ATT&CK mapping and corresponding mitigations list - hancitor" width="1200" height="1414"></noscript></picture></p>
</div>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    const postcontent = document.getElementById('blog-post-content');
    const h2counter = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2');

    for (i = 0; i < h2counter.length; i++) {
        const h2elementtag = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i];
        const h2element = postcontent.getElementsByTagName('h2')[i].innerHTML;
        let postnavitem = document.createElement('a');
        postnavitem.classList.add('post-navigation__item', 'subtitle2', 'scrollto');
        postnavitem.innerHTML = h2element;
        const postnavlink = postnavitem.innerHTML;
        let str = postnavlink;
        str = str.replace(/<[^>]*>/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\u0400-\u04FF]/g, '');
        str = str.replace(/\s+/g, '-').toLowerCase();
        str = str.replace(/^-+|-+$/g, '');
        postnavitem.classList += ' ' + str;
        postnavitem.href = '#' + str;
        h2elementtag.id = str;
        document.getElementById('post-navigation__wrapper').appendChild(postnavitem);
    }

    const sections = document.querySelectorAll('h2');
    const navItems = document.querySelectorAll('.post-navigation__item');

    window.addEventListener('scroll', () => {
        let current = '';
        sections.forEach((section) => {
            const sectionTop = section.offsetTop;
            const sectionHeight = section.clientHeight;
            if (pageYOffset >= sectionTop - sectionHeight / 10) {
                current = section.getAttribute('id');
            }
        });

        navItems.forEach((a) => {
            a.classList.remove('active');
            if (a.classList.contains(current)) {
                a.classList.add('active');
            }
        });
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrollto').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300,
        );

        return false;
    });

    // SMOOTH SCROLL WITH NO HASH
    var $root = jQuery('html, body');


    jQuery('a[href^="#"].scrolltonohash').click(function() {
        var href = jQuery.attr(this, 'href');
        var headerHeight = 100;

        $root.animate({
                scrollTop: jQuery(href).offset().top - headerHeight
            },
            300
        );

        return false;
    });
</script>

<script type="text/rocketlazyloadscript">
    function readingTime() {
        const text = document.getElementById("blog-post-content").innerText;
        const wpm = 225;
        const words = text.trim().split(/\s+/).length;
        const time = Math.ceil(words / wpm);
        document.getElementById("reading-time").innerText = time;
    }
    readingTime();
</script>

    function positioningSidebar() {
        let sidebar = document.getElementById('sidebar');
        let sidebarHeight = sidebar.offsetHeight;
        let windowHeight = document.documentElement.clientHeight;
        if (windowHeight < (sidebarHeight + 48)) {
            let sidebarAuthors = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__authors');
            let sidebarMorePosts = sidebar.querySelector('.sidebar__more-posts');
            let authorsHeight = sidebarAuthors ? sidebarAuthors.offsetHeight : 0;
            let morePostsHeight = sidebarMorePosts ? sidebarMorePosts.offsetHeight : 0;
            let sidebarTop = authorsHeight + morePostsHeight + 140;
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', -sidebarTop + 'px');
        } else {
            jQuery(sidebar).css('top', '72px');
        }
    }
    window.addEventListener('resize', positioningSidebar);
    positioningSidebar();

    if (localStorage.getItem('theme')) {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
        } else if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'light') {
            jQuery('body').addClass('theme--white');
        }
    } else {
        localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        jQuery('body').addClass('theme--dark');
    }

    jQuery('.theme-toggle').click(function() {
        if (localStorage.getItem('theme') == 'dark') {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'light');
        } else {
            localStorage.setItem('theme', 'dark');
        }
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--dark');
        jQuery('body').toggleClass('theme--white');
        setTimeout(() => {
            jQuery('body').toggleClass('no-transition');
        }, 250);
    });
    jQuery('.share-toggle').click(function() {
        jQuery('.share-toggle').toggleClass('active');
        jQuery('.share-widget').toggleClass('show');
    });
</script>
